ParlaMint-GR_2020-01-21-S1-commons.u3	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, I am addressing today from the Step of the Greek Parliament to all the young people of our country and to their parents who in recent years have been inflamed by their confidence in the state, to a state which has been sloppy and recklessly left to develop schools without professional rights, studies without an impact on the labour market that produces unemployed people with degrees, children who are ready to leave abroad to find their guidance, educational programmes without qualitative evaluation and scientific objects without prospects. I am addressing parents who have seen their children choose universities not on the basis of the professions of the future or the conquest of modern knowledge, but on the basis of fashion, because some of their classmates have chosen it or taboo because they have been cultivated that they should only do specific professions - doctors, lawyers, engineers - without selecting sectors of the top and bottom-up national economy that supports the country's GDP, such as shipping professions. This shattered relationship between youth and family on the one hand and the state on the other is trying to restore the government with this bill, considering it as the first fundamental step in a series of changes and reforms that will follow. Our aim is to train in qualityly evaluated programmes to successfully lead young people through the professions of the future to their immediate integration into the labour market and, at the same time, to conquer the real education that guarantees not only the high standard of living, but also a mature and fair society, because for us education is the aim of education. Education is something higher, more substantial, deeper and more complete than mere education. Education is a worthwhile code, national and cultural identity, intake of fresh knowledge and modern information, possession of modern know-how, self-consciousness and self-consciousness, skills development, correct choices of ways and directions, essential and typical preparation for integration into the Greek and international labour market with a feeling of self-confidence, with the ability to continuously improve the quality, ability and effectiveness of the new scientist. This draft law comes at a critical moment to respond to a series of agonizing searches for young people who are looking for their step forwards, parents who are struggling for the professional perspective of their children, the educational and academic community that is concerned about the curiosity, lightness, distortions, mistakes and omissions of the past. This bill does not only seek to abolish, but to correct and above all to supplement the institutional framework, by harmonising it with the European environment. Its main features are that it is not a faculty but a student. It proposes education, and not just education, by highlighting standards of excellence. It promotes the extroversion and internationalization of the Greek HEI, rather than their closure, isolation and ossification. It links funding to quality and efficiency assessment. It establishs the ongoing assessment, overcoming the idea of and the establishment of the mediterranean dictatorship. It adopts mobility in education and research, creating a direct conflict with the forces of inertia. It connects the education of all levels to the market, highlighting the national authority in a vehicle not only existing but also future curriculum, which will respond to the professions of the future, the professions of the peak, the needs of local communities hosting university institutions and the realistic expectations of our national economy. It implements Article 5A of the Cyber Access Rights Constitution. With the state IT certificate it hits digital illiteracy and facilitates preparation for the new era. It emits a strong social state signal, creates conditions of accessibility for SMEs, gender equality, the environmental footprint of institutions, and ensures luxury. It answers the key question: "Who is doing the research in Greece, who oversees it and how does it motivate it, the market, the state, the university or all together?" It establishes the development of a national educational strategy, which is coordinated by the state, the academic community, society and the market, which will respond to the needs of the labour market, to shield the worthy of our culture and to meet the national and international challenges of the future. The draft law followed the modern process and principles of good legislature. It entered public consultation between 4 and 18 December 2019, bringing together a hundred nine comments, taken into consideration. Completed with a explanatory statement, a table of amended and repealed provisions, a report from the General Accounting Office of the State, a specific report assessing the effect of the arrangements and a consultation report. In particular, it consists of sixty-four articles, which are legally structured into five chapters. Chapter I, in Articles 1 to 19, seeks to develop the old ADI to a modern National Higher Education Authority, which is fully upgraded, reorganised and aims at the reform of universities and their systematic and dynamic alignment with modern European data. The new organisational structure of the Authority shall be based on the Chairperson, the Board, the evaluation and certification board and the Director-General. The responsibilities of the National Higher Education Authority are considerably strengthened. Firstly, the establishment of the national strategy for higher education is being introduced, which must be compatible with the general national orientation of our country, the needs of Greek society and the potential of the national economy. Secondly, they are evaluated before, during and after universities, schools and departments, while it is decided to establish, merge, partition or abolish them if they do not lead to professional rights and real prospects in the economy. The main are specialities and fields that the Greek economy really needs. A criterion for the choice of children and their families, as I said, who now trust the state, is not the taboos of the past, nor the decision of the company, nor the city that offers good student life. It is the profession, it is science, which is the orientation, which will lead the child to complete education and facilitate integration into the national and international labour market. The state through the national authority must guarantee high-level studies, top sectors that complete the personality of the young person, meet the economic investment of its family and lead to peak professions that meet the needs of the Greek economy and of course the Greek society. Thirdly, for the first time, public funding without jeopardising the operation of traditional schools, which absorb 80% of their inappropriate expenditure on objective criteria, motivates universities to show positive mobility by giving them the 20% bonus. The criteria of this incentive enhance the quality, research activity, the internationalisation of higher education institutions, through their cooperation with foreign institutions, their association with the labour market, the increase, as is said, of the employability of their students, who are once they have graduated from the market. Fourthly, the Evaluation and Certification Council assesses all cycles before, during and after, and for the first time, thematic evaluations concerning internationalisation strategies, issues relating to gender equality, environmental footprint, accessibility of SMEs, the use of new technologies and other such criteria are also introduced. The dependence of 20% of public funding on all social, intelligent and international actions is blocking our universities from the power of inaction and creates a new mobility, a fertile speech and a rewarding extroversion. In Chapter II, and in particular in Articles 20 to 27, we are answering the key questions: 'Who is carrying out the research in Greece, the university or the market?' The university is given a unique opportunity to regain its own space, i.e. the area of fresh knowledge and modern information. The main feature of the remodeling is the combination of flexibility and speed on the one hand, with accountability and transparency on the other. I will not go into the details of the special accounts, because we will be able to analyse it in the debate on the articles. In Chapter III, for the first time, the State certificate of informatics is laid down in Articles 28 to 32. It specialises and actively strengthens Article 5A of the Constitution, which considers as equal access to access not only the possibility of having fibre or access to a wifi, not only the possibility of having a computer but also to know how to handle new technology. On substance, the certificate comes to widen the battle against digital illiteracy and cover this aspect of access, i.e. computer management knowledge, so that one can enter the internet and benefit from it. In Chapter IV, a series of loose ends, arising from misappropriations, mistakes or prejudices of the past, are resolved, but in no way will young people be affected by the decisions taken by the legislator. Article 33 suspends the training function of the thirty-seven departments established by the previous government, without respecting the evaluation process, with haste, roughness and perhaps even pre-electional feasibility. Article 34 deletes the religion and nationality of the certificates of studies and thus substantially complies with the Ministry's Decision 28 of the Personal Character Authority. Article 37 ensures the effective administration of the International University in a flexible and functional manner. Article 43 facilitates the admission of foreign students to Greek language courses. Language is power, it is the tool of extroversion, it is the instrument of foreign policy, it is the strengthening of the international Philatelic current. Article 46 ensures that trainees are moved to second-rate schools. It is an important aspect of social policy. Article 44 provides greater autonomy for the school unit for organising school tours. Article 50 restores compliance with the law of the European Union with regard to the recognition of professional qualifications and professional equivalence and equivalence. In Article 51, we guarantee the trust of students at the University of West Attica in the selection of completion of the curriculum. In Article 54, the possibility of examinations by a group of schools is abolished, while in Article 55 we are looking for a large family. Article 56 establishes the day of celebration of the Three Hierarchs, January 30, as a school celebration and not as a holiday. The reintroduction of the Three Hierarchs' feast is the restoration of a void caused by the previous government by its abolition. SYRIZA imposed a holiday. In essence, it abolished the celebration of the Three Hierarchs. He thus sought, satisfying the sense of the students to lose a lesson, to deny them the opportunity to be informed of the enlighteners of the Orthodox faith, who brought Orthodoxy closer to the Greek spirit. This attitude then condemned by the Church of Greece reveals their desire to impose a religiously indifferent state that allegedly invokes the principles of the religiously neutral state. Here also seems our qualitative difference from SYRIZA, who even in the Revision of the Constitution sought to abolish what has to do with Orthodox tradition and faith. Challenging and provoking, he even sought to identify our Church with the State, misinterpreting the term "predominant religion". And he deliberately ignored the fact that the term "predominant religion" does not identify the Orthodox Church and faith with the State, but matches him with the vast majority of the Greek people. In fact, in the committee, a SYRIZA colleague sought inadmissiblely to identify the decision to reinstate the Orthodox Enlightenment Enlighteners with Iran's extreme Islamic regime. But that, ladies and gentlemen, is our difference. We do not smell it or slander it to abolish it. We let it be free, as provided for in Article 13 of the Constitution for religious freedom, and we respect it in practice. But you, in ideology and prejudice, are fighting, questioning and slandering about the Orthodoxy and tradition of this people, ignoring the historical role played by the Church of Greece in all the national and social struggles of our nation. Article 58 requires the uninterrupted operation of school units, mainly in remote areas, by the refusal of teachers to fulfil their duties and has been placed there. Chapter E includes transitional and repealing provisions. This bill is a first attempt to defeat the forces of inertia and idealism that often ensue the most sensitive but also dynamic part of our society and state, which are no more than our youth and the educational world. The liberation of healthy forces, the defeat of the dictatorship of moderates, the return of excellence, the strengthening of quality and extrovertism can make our homeland an international educational centre, stressing the real economy, supporting the tested society and exploiting education as our national comparative advantage. Whatever national strategy we decide on, one is certain, ladies and gentlemen, that its root and foundation cannot be from education. Because it's all about education. Thank you very much.

In fact, however, the institutional framework is being changed. It changes the name, without anyone knowing why it is necessary, as well as how to choose the administration with the eruption of today's leadership, removing the unsatisfactory and unwilling to implement its policy to an absolute extent. Ladies and gentlemen, it is clear to us that the government has been acceding to the establishment of this new authority, which it calls the National Higher Education Authority, with a view to indirect manipulation and its full control. This testifies to the arrangement that strengthens the supervisory role of the Minister of Education, transforming the new IP into a strap of transferring government's higher education policy. At the same time that you theoretically transfer to the new authority increased responsibilities, such as evaluation, allocation of funding and design of the academic map, in the same way, invoking its own independence, you choose to avoid the political cost of the unpopular reforms that you promote for the Greek universities. One of the most upcoming, most unpopular arrangements is the change in the model of funding for higher education institutions, for which you have even rushed to state that you are ideologically proud. Proud, but as it turns out you will create serious financial problems in public universities and pass them on to reasonable and market-based practices as if they were business and not to the academic community. You have stated that the funding of the HEIs in the past was made at the request of the Minister. I refer you to L.4009/2011 and to Article 73, entitled ‘Summary of the public funding of S.H.I.’, where specific criteria and indicators are laid down. And of course I'll testify to it in the Minutes, although you know it. These are precisely the indicators you mention for 80% of the funding. And that's to finish half the truths and create impressions. Nor is there - because you said so - a decision of the Committee on Budgets, which was proposed to the previous leadership and not accepted. On the other hand, there had been working committees which had been set up at the initiative, but which did not reach any specific conclusions. According to your regulation, the financing of HEIs will be made on the basis of the general criteria, as was the case until now, 100%, and 20%, on the basis of private and non-private criteria. Here the new principle that has been introduced will play its part and will facilitate you in decisions, as I have said before. Where an IIT fails to achieve its quality and achievement indicators, the funding it will not receive as a consequence of the evaluation will be allocated to the financing of other higher education institutions and shall be defined as one of the tasks of that authority. Therefore, the criteria are not criteria for rewarding higher education institutions. They're punishing criteria. You said that we have no confidence and you have confidence in higher education institutions. So, get away from the suggestion made to you by the decision of the meeting of the Presidents, like many other congressmen, of the National Technical University, the Ioannina Declaration, multiple Ioannina Members' Associations, who consider that with existing sub-funding in the higher education institutions what you are proposing, will broaden the inequalities between higher education institutions, intensify internalisation, trigger internal conflicts in the country's scientific and research community. At the same time, there is no doubt that it will weaken the weakest universities, which are regional or some of the regional universities and will lead them to a search for resources outside the public purse. You know, Mrs Kerameou and Mr Digalakis, the universities are asking you to maintain last year's funding rates. Instead, you choose to enter market-applied funding models that trample academics and prescribe, for at least some of them, a 20% reduction in funding. Listen to the opinion of the universities! Share their anxietys and reservations! Focus on the real needs of universities, which are as strong as they can be with resources and staff, and I am pleased that you are saying this at least at the last minute - the one-to-one rule because I remember that here you were talking about rule 1 to 5, which we have legislated and which the higher education institutions have a great need. We stress once again that beyond our ideological approaches and our differences and internationally this model did not produce the expected results. That is why Europeans have turned to other policies through the European networks of universities, which have successfully started several of our own universities to be part of them and to move forward in this very important direction. I shall now proceed to the PPE. This second part relates to the PPE of the universities and research centres of Greece. One of your original commitments, if I'm not mistaken, was that you'd put them out of public account. You haven't achieved it. You're being strongly criticised. For our part, we believe that the introduction of PPE into the public accounting system is not a mistake in principle. Although their subjection was made to deal with phenomena of mismanagement, transparency and accountability, it did lead to a bureaucratic stature of thousands of academic researchers and managers. So the previous law, our own, was a first attempt to improve this framework of operation. Indeed, some of the proposed provisions are a continuation of this effort for a more flexible and effective operating framework. And so, in this type of provision, which simplifies cost-approval procedures and facilitates, we will be positive. In addition to these provisions, however, there is also Article 60 which provides for the legalisation of categories of expenditure to be effected by way of derogation from the legislation. Despite my constant questions to the committees, I have so far not been given convincing answers - which I expect in the current procedure - about the level and origin of expenditure. Of course, you are constantly referring to the fact that these served micro-political purposes, they did not have specific academic criteria, they had no prior feasibility studies, there was no consistent planning. We increased funding, set up institutions with IGC members and new researchers, restored rule 1 to 1 on withdrawals - recruitments, established academic rules at all levels of study and tried to deal with chronic distortions and, of course, to promote transparency in the management of funds. ParlaMint-GR_2020-01-21-S1-commons.u7	Thank you, Mr President. Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, we are debating a very important draft education legislation today, but the Ministry, unfortunately, is blurring its value. Continuing the practice of fragmenting measures on major issues within a squabble of dispersed, contradictory and unclear regulations, the Government - otherwise has education as a national priority - starting with its own brand name with a draft law cut off and based on PASOK's n.4009/2011. Of course, there is no shortage of'surprises' always 'other provisions', through which decisions are taken on serious, completely procedural, regardless of the subjects involved, which were to be the main body of another bill for primary and secondary education. After six months of governance of the New Democracy, the bill for the National Authority of Higher Education - ETHAE - and the Special Accounts - ΕΚΕ - of EIT aims at restoring transparency and strengthening the autonomy and accountability of higher education institutions. At the same time, it attempts to link the results of the evaluation to a part of the funding of the institutions. So these arrangements are not new. The concept of evaluation of the EITs of the country was laid down and applied in practice at the legislative initiative of the PASOK, with a landmark law, which was then embraced by almost all the political world and correspondingly "removed" quietly and methodically before it could be implemented. After historian time, SYRIZA fought the idea of evaluation, betting on minor effort, inappropriateness and ultimately in levelling. The questioning of the role of the IPD came as a natural result. In the work of two inspiring women of education ministers of two different political spaces, Marietta Giannakus with Law 3374/2005 and Anna Diamantopoulou with Law 40009/2011 only needed one term of "the first time left" to leave stains of dissolution and decline. Ladies and gentlemen, after the debate in the committees, the shortcomings, failures, hagglings and malfunctions have emerged, which lose one of the most valuable tools for the development of our country, education. So what should be done? This question seems not even to have been raised by the leadership of the Ministry of Education, in order to understand the need for national dialogue on education and the need to reconstitute the ERC, which we are constantly calling for. Without this concern, however, how do we expect answers and similar integrated legislative initiatives? And if I point out the fragmentation of the arrangements, it is because the lack of strategy, the lack of a global approach and an overall viewpoint has implications and is unfair to any attempt. We have often asked what the government's overall plan for education is, an integral part of which is NATIONAE, the EBRD and - since we put it all through - the school excursions, the holidays of the three Hierarchs, the certificates of language and colleges. Do you know what you've answered? You have referred us to the election brochure of the New Democracy. It is like saying to the House, and ultimately to the citizen, that 'I have already decided what to do and I do not listen to anyone here, and we are simply observing the pretexts'. Education is a national issue, however, and as such cannot be replaced by the election programme of any party. This is mainly because any election programme we have, however good it may be, is missing the basic element of democratic governance that is national dialogue. Madam Minister, ladies and gentlemen, our country and our universities can, and we dream of it, become a global education centre. But this, in order for it to become a reality, needs much more than scattered provisions. There is a need for a national education strategy over time. Undoubtedly, the most crucial issues raised in this bill are the establishment of the ETHAEC - to date IPPC - and the EBRD. So let's start from Chapter I - Articles 1 to 19 - on NGO. Madam Minister, I would also like to thank you personally on behalf of the Change Movement for incorporating the proposals we have put forward as a contribution to this first draft education bill. The role of NCAs as an Independent Authority should be strictly advisory. Is it really? Its task is to specialize in this strategy. What strategy is there? The establishment of a national strategy is and must be the task of the government following a national dialogue and the necessary convergences. The NEAFC cannot undertake this strategy unless it specialises and monitors its implementation by the universities. In this bill, the Ministry started backwards. It strengthens the external audit and supervisory body, without previously addressing the big issue of the management bodies themselves. Simple logic says we're starting from the beginning. First discussion on the university institutions themselves and then discussion on the external supervisory and control bodies. Otherwise, what self-interest are we talking about, ladies and gentlemen? This reversal of the beginning of the debate circumvents the constitutionally regulated self-government of universities. On the financing issues of the HEIs, which must not be forgotten that they have already suffered drastic reductions from the state budget, they have lost, without, unfortunately, a large part of their staff, while the invaders are growing, and we have also expressed serious reservations to the committee. I am referring to the famous article 16 on state grant for HSY. We do not understand how the state funding of 20% of the country's EITs will actually be implemented, when funding is conditional on fulfilment of criteria based on indicative indicators of quality and achievements that each EIT chooses to evaluate. It is absolutely questionable whether this grant, which requires a large number of procedures, will be possible to be granted in the financial year or in practice it will be a 20% reduction in the State grant to the HEIs, under the pretext of fault of the HEIs themselves rather than the Ministry. This is why the remaining 20% of the funding foreseen will be provided as long as the institutions achieve their objectives to be allocated, there should be a programme agreement between the Ministry of Education and the IMI, in which the objectives are set. In the second year and after the objectives have been set, an assessment should be made to assess whether they have been achieved. The bill provides for thematic evaluations of individual activities of the IITs, such as the absorption of graduates in the labour market. As has been pointed out, however, there is no comparative assessment, for example of the research work of similar sections, of the costs per student in similar sections, and so on. And, unfortunately, they will show the strengths and weaknesses of individual institutions or their units and they inform the public, students and students of the quality of the sections they choose. In our opinion, this means timely and valid accountability. Minister, when the evaluation is carried out properly and in accordance with procedures which institutions do not kneel with bureaucracy, it serves primarily the objective of discovering weaknesses and deficiencies to be dealt with by the IMIs with the state and not a punishment. The evaluation must be carried out in the form of a modular rather than a punishment. Thus, additional funding turns into a tool that helps universities to develop, strengthen and exploit their strengths, address their potential weaknesses, achieve their goals and provide quality education to their students. Of course, the assessment and certification of higher education by NISA should not only be exhausted on the side of universities. ParlaMint-GR_2020-01-21-S1-commons.u21	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, we are here today for the ratification of the government's draft law on education entitled 'National Higher Education Authority, Special Accounts for Higher Education Institutions, Research and Technology Bodies and other provisions'. This is, in fact, the first basic piece of legislation, which also covers the area of education, and which the Government of New Democracy, unfortunately, does not surprise us at all, but at the same time is particularly concerned, and I do not want to tell you that we are saddened with the fact that this is a bill that is moving within the framework and the logic of an enterprise or, if you prefer, an operational model. Unfortunately, the government neither escapes nor insults from its pervasive principles, the principles of commercialisation, the so-called excellence, the customer relations, the interdependent interests and brings to plenary a bill completely classic and a little toxic, which undermines the independence, autonomy and self-interest of university institutions, and at the same time aims at standardising education and subjecting it to market rules, thereby giving the right to education to the economic and social stronger than the Greeks. The Government of New Democracy is closely and precisely following the main direction of the European Union strategy for the establishment of the single higher education area, which, however, has been strongly questioned internationally, as its applied models have failed and have not only failed, but have dissolved where the higher education system has been implemented in other countries of Europe, such as the United Kingdom, Great Britain. So, in faithfully following the European Union's directives and guidelines, we believe that the ground for the gradual withdrawal of the state funding of university institutions and for their delivery ultimately to the completely uncontrolled market forces, with disastrous results for public, free and all higher education and obviously undermines and threatens the autonomy and self-interest of these universities. A key instrument of this policy, as shown by Articles 1 to 19, will be the establishment of a new over-concentration and a powerful authority, the so-called National Higher Education Authority, which is coming to replace the Independent Quality Assurance Authority in higher education. In our view, in the sense of Mera25, this change is, in fact, a renaissance that covers the real objective of the new principle, since, with the argument of the alleged upgrading, conditions and alibi are created to remove from their positions all the unsatisfactory in the present Government and thus to undermine the independence of this new principle from the beginning of its operation, a principle with more strengthened responsibilities, such as the strategic planning, financing and evaluation of the Higher Education Institutions. And while - with Article 2 - a small paradox - the evaluation and quality certification of the operation of the Higher Education Institutions falls within the competence of this new authority, under Article 12(8) - o of the miracle! - provision is made that quality certification may also be carried out by an accredited higher education certification body from abroad, defined by a decision of the Evaluation and Certification Council. This latter, coupled with the direct relationship that the government will have with the selection of the chairmen of the three-member committees chosen by the members of the Board of Governors, makes us think and have reasonable suspicion that the National Higher Education Authority will have no relationship with transparency and democratic procedures. The Higher Council with its powers makes the intervention of the higher management bodies of the HEIs, such as the Senate, for example, completely marginal, as it is the highest council that will be recommended - rather than the congresses or other bodies of the universities - to the Ministry of Establishment, Consolidation, Elimination or rename of entire HEIs or their individual schools. In accordance with Article 16, 80% of the regular grant will be allocated on the basis of criteria relating to the total number of students registered, the estimated annual cost of study per student, the duration of the curriculum and the size and geographical spread of the university institution, while the remaining 20% will be allocated on the basis of indicative indicators of quality and achievement, to which each HEI chooses to be assessed, such as the arithmetic relationship between the graduates and the incoming students, the number of members of the scientific staff who achieve funding from European funds or the number of foreign students. It is clear that there is a government's intention to ultimately reduce state funding, which, incidentally, is also a constitutional requirement, to grant our education, and the cost of our universities' operation, to be passed on to private bodies and, finally, to their own students, who will now be treated as customers. The implementation of this policy will be achieved through an evaluation which, in our view, has a purely punishable character, since 80% of funding will depend on objective criteria and the remaining 20% of funding from some doubtful performance criteria. 20%, therefore, of funding through evaluation theoretically provides incentives for the improvement of HEIs in targets that - supposedly - will be chosen themselves by strengthening their autonomy. However, as these performance criteria show, these criteria are already fully defined by the Ministry of Education and Religious Affairs and do not in fact leave any margin of autonomy to our higher education institutions. I think it is worth mentioning, on this point, a small example of the customer practice already in place by the government and, in our view, the marginalisation of the Greek public university. This example is nothing more than the recent sponsorship of the Greek state of two million euros that our Prime Minister at the Boston Theological School, in which I am also learning, by the way, that students pay an annual tuition of 20,000 euros. At the same time, where these two million euros are given as a donation to the Theological School, which, from its students, takes EUR 15,000 a year from each of them, Greek higher education institutions grow, do not have sufficient resources for research, educational or administrative staff, do not have adequate buildings, do not have adequate equipment, student benefits are completely inadequate and so on. Here, we believe, it is particularly important to stress that the impact of evaluation on social sciences, humanities and basic research on positive sciences will be overwhelming, since measurable sizes in these sciences cannot assess the quality of theoretical knowledge. In addition, knowledge and teaching process are provided and the character of the university is changed from knowledge, learning, research and exchange of ideas to a model of entrepreneurial education, which eventually turns the student into a customer and the university teacher to an employee. With Article 33, it is suspended indefinitely and without any consultation with the academic community - why, Madam Secretary and Minister, without consultation with the academic community? - the operation of the thirty-seven new departments and eleven universities foreseen by SYRIZA, the previous government, which would have operated since 2021 and which will now be assessed in unclear, in our view, by this new all-powered authority to decide whether or not these will eventually work. By amending Article 54(5) of Law 4589/2019, according to which diplomas awarded by foreign education institutions are accepted for appointment and recruitment of teachers, not only if they have recognition of academic equivalence and equivalence by the interdisciplinary organisation for the recognition of academic qualifications and information that has been in force so far, but even if they have only a professional qualification recognition decision. This implies that private college diplomas, which under Law 4635/2019 have a professional equivalent to the diplomas of Greek higher education institutions, are considered to be a formal qualification for permanent appointment and recruitment in public education. In addition, the following question is raised: We thus recognise qualifications which are not recognised in the State of origin of these diplomas. For example, in the United Kingdom, in order to be able to teach in public schools, one must have Qualified Teacher Status, something like pedagogical competence, that is, after some practical exercise. While Greece can only apply for appointment to the AACC with its degree, which it acquired from a university in the United Kingdom. Finally, it is about relaxing the criteria for selecting teachers in public schools with what this implies. In practice, also, this abolishes Article 16 of the Constitution from the back door, since beyond the symbolic burden, the term 'university', all the practical substance of the case of exclusive public and state universities and the prohibition of private universities is about whether a person with a college degree can be appointed to the public, and how much more so can it be appointed in public as an educator, as is now possible with this bill. Article 58 also stipulates the exclusion for a period of three whole years from appointments and recruitment for those teachers who are appointed and do not undertake a service or resignation within one year of their publication of the FEK. Teachers, in other words, taking part in the process of permanent recruitment, but not taking up duties for any reason or giving up in their first year of appointment, for the next three years and not only for the current year, will, as it would be reasonable, be excluded from both permanent appointments of alternates to primary and secondary education. ParlaMint-GR_2020-01-21-S1-commons.u23	Thank you, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, the draft law of the Ministry of Education and Religious Affairs, entitled "National Authority of Higher Education, Special Accounts for Higher Education Research Institutions, Research and Technology and other provisions, is currently being introduced to the Greek Parliament for discussion today. First of all, I would like to congratulate the Ministry of Education's political leadership and congratulate the impeccable parliamentary procedure that has been followed up to now and which is an example of good legislation. The proposed bill, after completing a consultation cycle from 4-12-2019 to 18-12-2019, was submitted to the Committee on Educational Affairs, where it was thoroughly examined by its members with the participation of the institutions involved. It consists of five chapters and sixty-four articles which contain important and necessary regulations, amendments and abolitions of provisions in order to deal with chronic pathogens and omissions, which act as a brake on the functioning, reliability and quality of the education of our universities. Chapter I includes the establishment and organisation of the National Higher Education Authority and innovative funding arrangements for HEIs. Replaces the PPP, the Quality Assurance and Certification Authority by the National Higher Education Authority, by redefining its organisation and functioning framework. The establishment of the new principle was deemed necessary in order to contribute to the implementation of the national strategy for higher education, the impartial evaluation and certification of the quality of the functioning of our Higher Education Institutions, the creation of safety controls of good functioning through administrative autonomy, the upgrading and strengthening of the responsibilities of the new authority, the integration of the best international practices, the establishment of new collective administrative bodies with increased independence guarantees, the extension and certification of postgraduate and doctoral programmes, to include those which had been interrupted, the definition of the evaluation criteria, some of which are access to people with disabilities, the internationalisation strategy, the absorption of graduates in the labour market, the development of e-learning programmes and many but. At the same time, it is attempting to revise the financing framework for HEIs in part. 80% of the regular GNI grant is allocated on the basis of the existing criteria. The remaining 20% will be allocated on the basis of quality and achievement indicators. Chapter II regulates the Special Accounts for Research Funding and Technology Bodies of the Research and Technological Institutes of the IITs. Research is liberated from the ideological strangles of the past and bureaucracy is reduced, while accountability and transparency are increased. They increase from 5% to 10% of the ERCB's cash assets to grant grants. It is laid down as a basic condition for the taking up and pursuit of the expenditure concerned or for not exceeding the authorised limit of the total budget of the project. It is also allowed to implement financial obligations irrespective of the amount, which will not exceed the limits of the approved budget and not the limits by the category of expenditure, as was the case to date and which has caused a great deal of bureaucratic problems. The process of monitoring the implementation and implementation of expenditure will be carried out on the basis of the rules laid down by a decision of the Minister for Education and Religious Affairs and not the Minister for Finance, as was the case until now. Chapter III provides for the establishment for the first time in our country of the State Certificate of Informatics. The State Certificate of Informatics will be obtained by exams and will certify the knowledge of computer manipulation in the use of internet services, text processing and computer sheets. His knowledge can be extended to other digital objects by decision of the Minister of Education and Religious Affairs. In Chapter IV, the following arrangements are summarised in the ‘other provisions’: the training function of thirty-seven new sections of HSI, which were established ‘in the knee’ by the previous government, without any evaluation of the relevant institutions and the IPO, as provided for by Law 4485/2017. The effective administration of the International University of Greece is ensured by a flexible and functional administrative structure. It is easy to attract foreign students to learn Greek. It also ensures that trainees are moved to the second opportunity schools who live away from the school's headquarters. Examinations by group of schools are abolished. Multitude teachers are part of the specific transfer categories, with a view to being transferred from region to region as well as to the boundaries of the same region. In addition, it shall ensure the smooth functioning of school units through exclusion for three years from recruitment and appointment of teachers appointed to primary and secondary schools, who either do not undertake a service or undertake and then resign within a year. This is mainly seen in mountainous and remote regions. Chapter E refers to transitional and repealed provisions. Ladies and gentlemen, the draft law under discussion is not a text of regulation and of opportunityal settlements. It is a legal text of high standards that fully reflects the ideology of the current government in both the quality of secondary education and the unbundling functioning of the independent authorities. It introduces innovations in higher education and research funding and greatly exploits the comparative advantages of our country to human and scientific resources. Within the framework of the Constitution, and given the ideological perception of the previous SYRIZA government about the non-revision of Article 16, for non-state universities, the present government is trying to achieve the best. The great goal of becoming our country's international education centre by establishing non-state universities with multiplier effects on the economy and society, once again lost. It is certain that the fair judge of all people in democracy, the people and history will judge everyone, especially those who, through their actions and their outdated and condemned world ideas, are holding the country close to their precarious choices. Finally, and after that, I am voting in principle and as a whole on the draft law. Thank you very much, Mr President.

ParlaMint-GR_2020-01-21-S1-commons.u39	Thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen, New Democracy and personally the Prime Minister of the country Kyriakos Mitsotakis have made an election commitment that the first priority of the new era of governance will be to form a new culture of progress in the main sectors of the country, such as education, the economy, national defence, etc. Key elements of this culture of progress will be strategic planning, evaluation and flexibility, three concepts which, while in all the states of the Western world are indeed the roots of society's progress at higher levels, unfortunately in our country these three very important concepts cause some negative reflexes in part of political staff. The first consequence of these negative reflexes is the conclusion of these concepts, such as, for example, that evaluation is a punishing process, and it is known that for the Government of New Democracy the assessment is purely an improvement. The second consequence of these negative reflexes is the absence of the concepts of strategic planning of evaluation and flexibility from the ground both of higher education and of all the main sectors of the country, as we saw in the previous government period. So what have we seen in the recent past in the field of higher education? We have seen the occasional establishment or merger of university departments due to political purposes, without any preparation and without even asking the competent independent authority. We have seen the absence of strategic planning for responding to new educational needs arising in various sectors of the economy and technology. It was particularly characteristic of the previous point that, despite the fact that since the previous political leadership of the Ministry of Education, some sixty university departments were established, not one of them healed the subject of the Data Science, as well as Artificial Intelligence, an object that is the basis of the fourth industrial revolution. What else did we see? We have seen a catastrophic bureaucracy in how university research is financed. What I am referring to now is just some phenomena that we have seen in the previous period, some phenomena that our role is to change immediately, because speed is something very important. The bill, therefore, of the Ministry of Education and Religious Affairs launches a new era in higher education, on the basis of this new culture of progress defined by the concepts "strategic planning", "assessment" and "flexibility". The regulations of the bill concern the evaluation and quality assurance of both the curriculum and the HEIs, the development of a long and coherent national strategy for higher education, the reduction of bureaucracy in the way specific research funds are operated, the well-known PPE, which are a central tool for financing research carried out at higher education institutions. In particular and very briefly: The strategic planning is implemented through the establishment of a new authority, the National Authority for Higher Education, with members of the prestige of the university community, both internal and external. This authority is responsible, among other things, for proposing to the Minister of Education the programme of the national strategy for higher education, namely, to which areas we will give priority and therefore, which programmes will be developed, and which national cooperation we will develop. It also proposes the establishment, merger, division, abolition of universities, schools, departments following rational analysis and objective academic criteria, and also the allocation of the annual state grant budget to HEIs by 80% on the basis of objective criteria - such as the estimated cost of studies per student, the total number of students, the size, and the geographical spread of an institution - and by 20% on the basis of the achievement of specific objectives set by the HEIs themselves, so that they can be assessed. I would like to say at this point that, in fact, this model of university funding is indeed innovative for our country. However, it is well known that it is a well-known and successful model, followed by countries with educational systems with huge university traditions such as Great Britain, Austria and not just. Furthermore, the NISA will, among other things, assess the IHS and whether they fulfil the conditions for being able to organise undergraduate, postgraduate and PhD programmes - and here I stress - regardless of whether these curriculum programmes are already established or planned to be established. Why am I saying that? Because in the very recent past, we have seen a series, as I mentioned, of many university programmes, which were set up or merged without any academic criterion, because of purely political purposes. Finally, flexibility in the PPE is to eliminate the bureaucratic tangles of the existing framework for the management of research funds, which forced us to be scientifically responsible - I remember it very strongly - to reform all academic teachers and Greek researchers constantly in the budgets as scientific managers. What he is doing is also a radical simplification of the budgetary procedures concerning the analysis and implementation of expenditure. Speaking in very simple terms, it is the word 'flexibility' to abolish counterproductive bureaucratic processes in the PPE Groups, which have essentially led to the reduction of the effectiveness and creativity of our research groups and, obviously, to the loss of jobs of very high-trained scientific staff. To close, without wanting to waste time, I would like to stress that the struggle of the Government of New Democracy to recover the important public university, the important role it had, continues. I have said and said again that we owe this to our students, to their parents, to society itself. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-GR_2020-01-21-S1-commons.u41	Thank you, Mr President. Madam Minister, Minister, ladies and gentlemen, higher education in Greece has undergone many changes, depending on the political agenda of the government concerned. However, we must all realise that education must be a sign of convergence of all the political forces. The basic guidelines of the education system are crucial to remain unaffected by the political changes and any changes that can be made only in the light of what the educational process really needs. Today's bill is no longer a theoretical account for higher education. It recommends the fulfilment of the commitments of the Government and Prime Minister in relation to the needs of the Greek universities. The first step was taken by amending the provisions on university asylum and restoring legality and regularity within university spaces. Today we are legislating on changing attitudes at the heart of the functioning of higher education institutions. Those who have studied in Greek universities know that these have excellent teachers, with a high level of academic education and training. However, this reality is not recognised abroad, because Greek universities do not take a good place in the assessments made at global level. Indicatively, according to the classification of "THE" for 2019, the Economic University of Athens is located in the range of positions "601-800", a little better than the university of Baghdad warfare. The bill under discussion lays the foundations for substantially upgraded Greek universities and for them to gain their proper place. The National Higher Education Authority ensures that the national strategy for higher education is systematically drawn up and that universities are in a continuous process of assessing their functioning. The High Council, which will operate within the new national authority, is responsible for collecting the necessary statistics and recommends to the Minister of Education the distribution of 20% of the UI grants. The Evaluation and Certification Board shall undertake the evaluation of universities and their certification that they fulfil the criteria for organising first, second and third cycle curricula. In fact, the Council also certifies the new curriculum, before they enter into operation, a procedure which is very necessary to ensure the level of the new curriculum before they are implemented. Also important are the evaluation of HEIs by themes, in the areas of internationalisation, gender equality, access to the labour market, environmental footprint. In all these issues, most Greek universities have great scope for improvement and the process of recording problems will help to improve the quality of the HEIs. In fact, in the Committee responsible for external evaluation and certification, a student representative will also be involved, making students part of this important process. The most preposterous change introduced by the bill is that in relation to how university funding is financed. To date, the responsibility for the allocation of funding lies exclusively with the Minister of Education. The new regulations define an objective and fair system. 80% of the grant will come from a combination of objective criteria, such as the number of students, the annual cost of study, the duration of the programmes, the size of the institution. However, 20% of funding for the first time will result from the indicators of quality and achievement, which are specific to the quality of the education process, the research activity of the university and its internationalisation. Thus, universities have a strong incentive to strengthen the areas in which they are lagging behind in order to obtain a positive assessment and to achieve as high a grant as possible. Another positive step is taken with centres of excellence. In particular, they will be characterized as centres of excellence by universities or parts thereof which are appreciably distinguished from the rest and which are qualitatively distinct in important areas. One of the most important changes in today's bill is that of the PPE, the special research funds. To date, the system of the EPPs has been extremely difficult, to an extent disappointing. On the contrary, research as a living process also requires flexibility in order to produce the expected results. The new regulations unleash the EPPs from the intolerable bureaucratic suffering, since the budget of each project is now self-evident in relation to the rest of the projects. At the same time, the budget of each EUPM shall be revised every month, subject to compliance with the overall budget. Finally, individual expenditure shall be considered legal when each project is carried out within the budget and not in any case within the limits of each category of expenditure. An innovation in today's bill is the establishment for the first time of the State Certificate of Informatics, since knowledge of computer usage is considered to be a minimum qualification for every professional employment. The other arrangements in the bill are once again the government's practical support for multitude families. Multitude teachers will be able to transfer to priority, both within the same region and from region to region. In conclusion, I would say that the Greek universities will strengthen their extroversion and have an international character with special programmes, but also with the strengthening of research, which will not for the first time be framed by the daring bureaucratic formalities. Modern Greek universities finally acquire the facilities to innovate and research in all sectors and not just form areas of higher education, but also places of production and promotion and education for the young citizens of our country. Thank you.

The responsibilities of this national authority are therefore enriched in three very important areas: strategic planning, financing of higher education institutions and their evaluation. In particular, the Higher Council of the National Authority is recommending to the Minister of Education a four-year national strategy programme for higher education, which is the first time being done. It also recommends the establishment, merger, abolition of school departments, universities, monitoring - and this is very important - the transition of graduates to the labour market, proposing proposals to ensure high quality in higher education, but also, of course, dealing with the allocation of the annual grant of higher education institutions. In the financing of HEIs, an important innovation is taking place, 80% will now be distributed on the basis of objective criteria, while the remaining 20% is linked to the Foundation's quality indicators. It is therefore possible for educational institutions to be the incentive to draw on these resources, to improve the areas of research, extroversion and, of course, the quality and effectiveness of education. A word about Article 50. I think that since 2005, by a directive of the European Union, foreign graduates, recognised by IATP, have been provided for in a European Union directive to be able to take part in IATP tests. For four years the SYRIZA government has allowed this to exist. Just before the elections, apparently for tiny reasons, the law abolished this process. That is why we are of course voting in favour of it.

ParlaMint-GR_2020-01-21-S1-commons.u118	Thank you, Mr President. The universities have shown the comparative advantages of the place through research, and the state has exploited them by investing politically and also resources on them, and that is so simple. Higher education in a country is an indicator of social, economic and political development and progress. The bill before us today intervenes on key issues of higher education. It goes on to set up an independent authority responsible for formulating the national strategy for higher education. At the same time it solves years of problems of the PPEC by creating a more flexible and adaptable operating framework, addressing the increased bureaucracy resulting from the problematic implementation of public accounting. At international level, the idea of university quality evaluation came from it precisely in the need to link them to the market and the sources of funding. In the United States, there is a voluntary evaluation programme for universities, while in many European countries such as England, the Netherlands and Sweden there are institutionalised independent authorities responsible for the evaluation of institutions. International evaluation body Times Higher Education ranks leading universities in Europe based on the quality of teaching. The evaluation focuses on subjects of study, teaching staff, quality of the institution's services, opportunities to develop skills, research staff productivity and others. Ladies and gentlemen, the truth is that in Greece yesterday in many areas of public administration, operation in fact of the state and its structures, the word has remained unknown. And I understand it, the truth is in this Chamber today with the posts of SYRIZA colleagues I have heard since the morning. In particular, in higher education evaluation is a prerequisite for ensuring rational development. Today we are responding to the needs of the times by directly linking the evaluation of universities with part of the state funding they receive. This is an internationally tested measure, and not a punishing process, as you have described it poorly, ladies and gentlemen of the SYRIZA. The absence of central planning and the opportunity, almost a customer way of doing things, let us tell the truth, have always been the path of higher education. The recent provisions of the Gavroglou Law through which thirty-seven new school departments were set up without any planning have best highlighted the need for an independent authority, a authority that will provide scientific and academic documentation on the map of the country's higher education. The National Higher Education Authority set up by the bill to be passed serves this very purpose. Our government stands beyond and far from the micro-political interests and logic that each village and TEI wanted, every city and university. I am reminded of the time-consultation between Minister and local bodies on the request for the creation of an independent University of Central Greece. It is also a given fact that the development of Europe's strong regions has relied on the functioning of universities operating at their place. It is therefore a fair request. The question, however, is who or who, and above all, on what criteria, will consider the viability of the proposal. Local bodies may say what they politically believe. But are they objective? Is it the job of local bodies, MPs or ministers to strategically direct the development of universities in our country? No, ladies and gentlemen. For these reasons, for my part, I welcome the Government's proposal to establish the Independent Higher Education Authority, because I am sure it will review the need for an autonomous university structure in the Region of Central Greece, based in Lamia. Because it is our duty to prevent the evasion of minds - not only from the large cities abroad - but also from the region to the large metropolitan centres. ParlaMint-GR_2020-01-21-S1-commons.u141	Thank you very much, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, the proposed arrangements of the Ministry of Education and Religious Affairs bill are in full agreement with the election commitments of Mr Mitsotakis. I am here admonishing that for us, this is praise and not cold. These are commitments concerning the transformation of higher education institutions into pillars, both locally and at regional level, that will develop and improve cooperation and networking with the world of production and entrepreneurship, with social actors and with internal and external institutions. This is the first time that it is attempting to create an innovative national education strategy, which is in synergy and good cooperation with the overall national development strategy, as well as with the strategy for the development of local societies and economies, moving the IPP to a National Higher Education Authority. It finally sets the guarantees of an objective assessment of all departments, schools and universities, and links with development and harmonisation criteria in the European environment some of the state funding. These criteria have always been a matter of concern, especially in the years of the crisis. The interconnection of education and vocational certification with the labour market - and for us, it is necessary and honest - the quality of teaching staff and services, which this offers trainees, the safeguarding and the competitiveness of professional rights throughout Europe and the extroversion - internationalisation of academic institutions. In addition, this bill comes to answer a key question, which the advanced countries have been clearing up for decades and are moving firmly and strategically. Who's investigating? This legislative initiative selects a mixed collaborative model. The Ministry of Investment and Development, to which the General Secretariat for Research and Technology belongs, ensures, by virtue of specialisation and know-how, the innovation and the development of research, while the relevant Ministry of National Education and Religious Affairs is monitoring the substance of the research and its compatibility with the country's society and the labour market. How will all this happen? Firstly, by strengthening and exploiting the institutional tool available to the state, i.e. the National Higher Education Authority, to upgrade and improve the quality of public HEIs, in a rapid, decisive, effective and modern way. This is evidenced by the adoption of new provisions, in addition to the existing institutional framework, of which provisions under the responsibility of the previous Government were only selective and not fully applicable. It should be recalled that the full implementation concerned provisions for the implementation of a national strategy, for the financing of institutions by means of programme contracts and for actions to establish and finance centres of excellence in the HEIs. These provisions in this draft law are strengthened and placed as part of a strong and coherent institutional framework that surrounds the functioning of the new authority. We will also achieve this by attempting to rationalise and structure the most unreasonable and unrealistic restructuring of the map of higher education, in which the previous government has made progress not only through the creation in a night of new institutions, departments and research structures, but also with the simultaneous abolition of existing at the expense of quality, science and academic values. This project is entrusted to the NRAE, which uses expertise and international experience in the quality systems of higher education as a method for setting up the necessary solutions or proposals. It should be noted that this reform of the Charter, in particular, raised a serious issue of the composition and functioning of the management bodies of the former PPP, since it created incompatibility with the members of the Board with what the existing institutional framework envisaged. Therefore, the need for the reconstruction of the management bodies of the former PPP was firstly submitted for the purpose of restoring legality and then for the purpose of extending its powers. In addition, the proposed provisions seek to radically reform the procedures and the financing framework of the HEIs by applying for the first time a link between a part of the state funding of universities with evaluation and with a central aim of continuously improving the quality of their operation. A more flexible and adaptable framework is therefore established for the operation of the specific research funds accounts, which are the central tool for research funding carried out in higher education institutions, as well as in research and technology bodies. Ladies and gentlemen, it is time for our country to cover the gaps in performance and finally to show the image of an organised and modern landscape in the field of higher education for the benefit of our students, their families and our national economy, for the benefit of the labour market and society, while being worthy of the respective international higher education systems. To this end, the new law of the Ministry of Education makes a decisive contribution to the strengthening and restructuring of the independent authority, which did not include the law on the State's performance. The time has therefore come for this gap to be completed quite soon and they deserve warm congratulations to the Minister, Mr. Niki Kerameos, to the State Secretary responsible, Mr. Digalakis, as well as to the officials. Finally, I do not want to fail to praise the right decision on the Three Hierarchs' festivities. Thank you very much.

The first and decisive step is the creation of this new, modern National Higher Education Authority, which replaces the Quality Assurance and Certification Authority, this principle that SYRIZA himself had degraded, since he never gave an opinion on the creation of any of the sixty-one more than sixty sections that he founded on his days, as the Undersecretary, Mr. Diagalakis, said. That authority shall be provided with broad competences enabling it to function effectively and to fulfil its mission with sound independence guarantees, while incorporating best international practices. The new principle will lay the foundations for the reform of the framework law, with a view to strengthening the self-employed and continuing assessment and the extroversion of universities. Unfortunately, in recent years we have witnessed a state planning for our educational institutions, without academic criteria, without feasibility and sustainability reports - that is a reality, ladies and gentlemen of the SYRIZA, no matter how disturbing you - rather than always synthesizing with micro-party interests and election purposes. Our Government with this bill dares and innovates, as the introductions certify curriculum programs prior to their start-up, considering whether universities actually fulfil quality requirements to organise all their curriculum and are linked and depended for the first time on part of the state funding from their evaluation, as will be analysed below. The aim is always its continuous quality upgrade. In the same direction as the continuous improvement of this quality of the Greek universities, in which we have all studied and know how many and what things are needed, there are important improvements in the field of research in the functioning of the specific research funds accounts, which is the main financial instrument of our university research. These accounts have been said many times, they are dysfunctional, everyone who is in any way related to them and to education, and most of the time they make research work difficult, both from universities and research bodies. With the new regulations, they become more flexible to promote not only scientific research, but also to ensure that special research resources are not lost. Proved, therefore, our Government defends the public university, promotes its quality upgrade and essentially supports not only the educational but also the very important research work it produces. Also, many necessary areas are also involved in individual procedural matters for the operation of school units, and all these changes take place in the direction of modernising efficiency, simplifying procedures and strengthening the autonomy of school units. These changes demonstrate our ideological faith in the selflessness of the school unit and our confidence and support in the role of the teacher. The new higher education authority will have a new structure and will be governed by new principles of operation. It will consist, as has been said, of three institutions whose choice is made following a public notice and the President, chosen by the Committee on Institutions and Transparency of Parliament, namely in conditions of complete transparency, impartiality and integrity. This purely remarkable structure, which strengthens the independence and autonomy of higher education institutions, does not understand why it disturbs SYRIZA. It is obvious that it is disturbed because it has allergy in any sense of evaluation and excellence, both public structures and the institutions of the country. The supreme Council of this principle will recommend to the Minister the programme of the national higher education strategy, this famous national strategy which we have been discussing for so many years and we still have not been able to implement. Because we will have a national strategy programme for higher education, which we will also implement. It will also recommend the establishment, merger, division and abolition of universities and schools, the annual programming agreements between the Ministry of Education and HEI, the allocation of the annual grant budget to the HEIs in the way it was said, i.e. 80% of state funding on the basis of objective criteria, the number of students, the geographical spread and 20% of state funding based on indicative quality indicators, to which each HEI will choose to be assessed. This is another important innovation, namely, the higher education institution itself to choose the indicative indicators to which it will be assessed. As has been said, these indicators concern the effectiveness of the educational process, the research activity that this institution must have and the internationalisation of the institution. Only if the quality indicators, which the higher education institution itself itself - I insist on - are not met, has it chosen, then only the amount corresponding to 20% of the grant will be allocated to the grant of other institutions. This prediction has caused SYRIZA's reaction, which prejudges that universities will lose this 20% because they will not catch the quality indicators. Why is this condemnation of the failure of our institutions, ladies and gentlemen, at first instance? This is because, in fact, SYRIZA is the one who underestimates and does not believe in the Greek university, unlike what he seems to support. He further argues that this will create inequalities and conflicts between universities, as other institutions will have achieved the indicators and not. However, we think it is very important that the educational institution itself chooses the area it wants to assess, precisely because we believe in the autonomy of the public university. And precisely because we believe in the Greek public university, we believe that if all the institutions operate in an organized manner, following the rules of merit and transparency that we are putting in place, they will make use of the procedural facilities and will be able to achieve all the quality indicators that they have chosen and thus receive the entire state grant. It is the Board of Assessment and Certification that will assess the educational institutions in general, but also, in particular, whether they meet the quality requirements required, to organise first, second and third cycle courses. It will also certify the quality of these programmes. At this point I would like to highlight two very important innovations introduced by the bill. For the first time, the curriculums to be established before they start operation will be assessed, on the basis of objective educational criteria. Because, unlike SYRIZA, which was abolishing educational institutions without any evaluation, we are setting evaluation and transparency rules at all stages of the establishment and operation of higher education institutions. In this context, the start of training of the thirty-seven new departments is suspended in the eleven schools established by the SYRIZA Government without any evaluation, bypassing, as we said earlier, the relevant procedure that SYRIZA itself had passed. Besides, you here too, through your representative, admitted that they had not even completed in many of these institutions the process of recruiting the necessary scientific teachers for their operation. Of course, this is not a 'outside' dismantling of them, as you say, misleadingly and for reasons of impression, but these sections will be assessed on the basis of precise objective criteria by the national authority, the sustainability studies will be carried out and, if all of this fulfils the criteria, they will function properly. The government finally puts an end to the micro-political tactics that have troubled the field of higher education over the past few years. Thematic and comparative assessments of institutions in fields such as gender equality, accessibility of disabled people, e-learning and internationalisation are also established for the first time. These areas are precisely about fundamental, individual and social rights and are at the heart of our policy. We do not want them to be the basic parameters of the new culture that we create in higher education. In particular, we consider the interventions made by this bill to be particularly important to individual issues that in practice demonstrate our social sensitivity and priority to social policy. We are introducing, for the first time, a State Certificate of Informatics, specifying Article 5, which considers as equal the provision and certification of knowledge and eliminating digital illiteracy, as our rapporteur rightly said. We support school education in remote or border regions, establishing a three-year exclusion from the appointment and recruitment of teachers appointed in primary and secondary schools and not taking up service or taking up service and resigning within a year of publication of the FEK. We are thus aiming to eliminate the unacceptable phenomenon of those appointed to the remote regions either not to take up service, or to waive their appearance within a short period of time. This will avoid malfunctions in the conduct of the courses and pupils will enjoy training on the same terms and conditions as other areas. We further take care of the protection of the multitude family, because this draft law proposes the integration of multitude teachers into the specific categories of transfer under Article 13 of Presidential Decree 50/1996, so that they can now be transferred from one region to another. For us, colleagues and fellow Members, the constitutional requirements for the protection of individual rights, such as the constitutional requirement for the protection of the multinational family referred to in Article 21 of the Constitution, are not just a proclamational one, but they are made every day and at every opportunity.
